{
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus",
  "section": "discussion",
  "old_text": "\\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics. First, $C_0$ (multi-candidate rate) combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling. At Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical for instruction-tuned models at $K = 12$), traces beyond 12 add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method---a result that applies to SC itself, not only to SICA. Together, these diagnostics allow practitioners to determine, before any constraint extraction or trace augmentation, whether additional test-time compute is likely to yield returns.",
  "new_text": "\\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method."
}

{
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus",
  "section": "discussion",
  "old_text": "\\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} Our experiments span 7B--32B parameters, but the method-level interventions (ICG, Contrastive, Debate) that test the independence-accuracy trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting (Debate) may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb. Whether 14B+ or 70B+ models can simultaneously maintain reasoning quality under independence-promoting interventions is an open question. The independence-accuracy trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but the location of the Pareto frontier may shift with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors (data-driven independence), external provers (tool-augmented independence), and multi-model pipelines (ensemble-based independence).",
  "new_text": "\\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines."
}

[Director 指令] [Patch] exp-013 Constraint Ablation 结果，加入论文 analysis section：

**数据**（n=804, FOLIO-204 + PW-600 pooled）：
- MaxSAT accuracy: 42.3%

- Random-weight accuracy: 42.1%
- SC accuracy: 43.2%
- MaxSAT vs count-only agreement: 94.4% (45/804 disagreements, MaxSAT 赢 18 vs count 赢 17)

- PW (Type C): MaxSAT 36.5% < count 37.2% < SC 39.3%

42	Four additional analyses confirm the mechanism underlying SICA's failure. Extraction error taxonomy (Appendix~\ref{app:extraction}): 91.8\% of constraints are technically discriminative, but their discriminative direction mirrors the majority answer; removing the 8.2\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero improvement. Constraint ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}): MAX-SAT and count-only baselines agree on 803/804 predictions, confirming degeneration to vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Hyperparameter sensitivity (Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}): $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\Delta > 2$\,pp among five $K$ values, and only $T = 0.7$ among four temperatures yields a positive effect; both peaks coincide with relatively low SC baselines rather than reflecting hyperparameter effects per se. Entropy decomposition (Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}): all SICA effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($\Delta$ up to $+12.8$\,pp in the intermediate-SC region), but the direction depends on the accuracy regime---high entropy signals correctable uncertainty only when the model has partial knowledge.
43	
44	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
45	\label{sec:remediation}
46	\label{sec:interventions}
47	
48	We test twelve remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) and three method-level (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). All nine prompt-level strategies fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\% but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models extracting from stronger traces yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models extracting from weaker traces produce accuracy within $\pm$1\,pp of self-extraction. Only 1 of 4 temperature settings yields a positive effect ($p = 0.092$); multi-temperature sampling produces $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
49	
```

2. Key Technical Concepts:

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe evaluated Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 instances with four-seed replication. Grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem, our analysis explains why SC majority voting saturates and why symbolic constraint aggregation fails to break this plateau under same-model self-extraction at the 7B--14B scale.\n\nThe primary finding is a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ generalization across aggregation methods reveals a Pareto frontier: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (Eff-$K$: $2.0 \\to 4.4$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO ($-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA); methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$). For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy provides a zero-cost diagnostic predicting when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help.         "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe oracle experiments causally identify the failure source. Holding all pipeline components fixed and varying only constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), while the best self-extraction captures only 24.1\\% of this ceiling. Because the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap localizes entirely to extraction quality.\n\nTrace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \\emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\\kappa$ generalization analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. Diverse-Prompt achieves $\\kappa = 0.40$ ($\\approx$ SC's $0.44$): prompt variation does not break the correlation. Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} No positive SICA effect reaches $p < 0.05$. The four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, negative effects are significant: $\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp ($p = 0.014$) and $-2.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.011$). Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). The converging evidence rests on the pattern across analyses, not any single $p$-value.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.",

        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this to a degree, but the core limitation persists.\nTrained reward models \\citep{lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd} and diversity-aware sampling \\citep{diverseaware2026} break the plateau by injecting external signal, but require trained verifiers or sampling modifications.\nA more fundamental question remains: can \\emph{structured aggregation} over symbolic representations extract information from the same traces that majority voting cannot?\n\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods suggest that formal reasoning can complement LLM generation.\nLogic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \\citep{olausson2023linc}, DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, and LogicAgent \\citep{luo2025logicagent} each report gains on logical reasoning benchmarks, but introduce components beyond the reasoning model: trained verifiers, solver selection, or external knowledge.\nA simpler question precedes these extensions: can the \\emph{same} model that generates reasoning traces also extract useful formal constraints from them?\nSelf-extraction would be a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model, training data, or external prover.\nYet no prior work has systematically evaluated whether self-extracted constraints are useful for aggregation.\nThis motivates our study of Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract FOL constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K$ grows \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails, as \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show for correlated voters, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nWe measure inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} and find values from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned conditions (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding Eff-$K = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nAt Eff-$K \\approx 2$, traces beyond $K = 12$ add negligible independent information---a scaling ceiling that applies to SC itself, not only to symbolic aggregation.\nWhen the same model both produces traces and extracts constraints, the constraints inherit the traces' biases \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, kambhampati2024position}.\nWe term this the \\emph{independence bottleneck}: self-extracted constraints cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires for aggregation to surpass SC.\n\\citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} examine CJT violations in LLM ensembles, but no prior study connects this framework to symbolic constraint extraction.\n\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 instances with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nThe central finding is a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off: $\\kappa$ generalization across aggregation methods shows that Debate reduces inter-trace $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15, tripling Eff-$K$, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nThis trade-off constrains all same-model trace aggregation, not only symbolic approaches.\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: gold FOL yields $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only 24\\% of this ceiling.\nTwelve remediation strategies (nine prompt-level, three method-level) all fail.\nA directional positive trend occurs in one model-dataset condition (Mistral-7B on FOLIO, 2 of 4 seeds with $p < 0.10$, none reaching $p < 0.05$; best $+3.43$\\,pp), but only in a narrow band around 50--55\\% SC; outside it, constraints echo the majority (Type~A) or amplify errors (Type~C).\nThe bottleneck is structural at the 7B--14B scale tested; whether larger models or trained extractors can overcome it remains open.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off (primary).} Fleiss' $\\kappa$ generalization across three aggregation methods reveals a fundamental Pareto frontier: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (Eff-$K$: $2.0 \\to 4.4$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO ($-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA); Diverse-Prompt preserves accuracy but cannot break the correlation floor. Twelve remediation strategies (nine prompt-level, three method-level) all fail, confirming that same-model aggregation cannot simultaneously achieve independence and accuracy (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Independence Bottleneck and Diagnostic Tools.} Same-model traces yield Fleiss' $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ (Eff-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for $K = 12$), quantifying SC's own scaling ceiling: Eff-$K \\approx 2$ means traces beyond $K = 12$ add negligible independent information for any aggregation method. The multi-candidate rate $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy provides a zero-cost deployment diagnostic predicting when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}, \\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Oracle-Extraction Gap.} Oracle constraints yield $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$). Self-extraction captures only 24\\% of this ceiling, localizing the failure to extraction quality, not the symbolic approach (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Regime-Dependent Pattern (directional).} A directional positive trend occurs in a narrow band ($\\sim$50--55\\% SC) in one model on one dataset (best $+3.43$\\,pp, mean $+1.47$\\,pp, none reaching $p < 0.05$). Three failure modes---confirmation bias, non-formalizability, error amplification---explain the regime boundaries (\\S\\ref{sec:main-results}).\n\\end{enumerate}",
        "numLines": 45,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 45
      }
    },

        "content": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces is the dominant test-time aggregation strategy for LLMs, but accuracy gains saturate as sample count grows. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) explains this plateau: same-model traces are correlated voters, so additional samples carry diminishing independent information. Symbolic constraints in first-order logic (FOL) appear to offer a way forward, but when extracted by the same model, they inherit the traces' correlation structure. We term this the \\emph{independence bottleneck}. We study it through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts FOL constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT. Evaluating six models (7B--32B) across three reasoning domains with over 3,000 instances and four-seed replication, we find that self-extracted constraints fail to surpass majority voting. A directional positive trend (best $+3.43$\\,pp, mean $+1.47$\\,pp; $p < 0.10$ in 2 of 4 seeds, none reaching $p < 0.05$) appears only in one model on one dataset at 50--55\\% SC baseline. Oracle controls with gold FOL confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound, yet self-extraction captures only 24\\% of the oracle ceiling. The central finding is a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off revealed by Fleiss' $\\kappa$ generalization across aggregation methods: Debate reduces inter-trace $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15, tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA. Methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. This trade-off constrains all same-model trace aggregation, not only symbolic approaches. The $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling, and $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy provides a zero-cost deployment diagnostic. The bottleneck is structural at the 7B--14B scale tested; whether larger models or trained extractors can overcome it remains open.",

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/analysis.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThe regime-dependent pattern in \\S\\ref{sec:main-results} establishes \\emph{where} SICA succeeds and fails. This section investigates \\emph{why}: trace correlation quantifies the SC plateau (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), supporting diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}), and remediation analysis reveals a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode reaches $\\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$). The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.8\\text{--}2.2$), yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence, drives the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ (Qwen conditions with $K_{\\text{eff}} \\leq 1.30$ never improve) and sufficient SC accuracy (Mistral on ProofWriter with the highest $K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ but SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Even when both hold, sampling variance determines whether the constraint pool is discriminative.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s42) & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostic Analyses}\n\nFour additional analyses confirm the mechanism underlying SICA's failure. Extraction error taxonomy (Appendix~\\ref{app:extraction}): 91.8\\% of constraints are technically discriminative, but their discriminative direction mirrors the majority answer; removing the 8.2\\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero improvement. Constraint ablation (Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}): on Mistral-7B ($n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter pooled), MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\\% of predictions, with disagreements split nearly evenly (18 vs.\\ 17); the solver's weighted satisfiability computation adds no value over simple constraint counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Per-domain, MAX-SAT provides a marginal edge in the intermediate-SC region (59.3\\% vs.\\ count-only 56.9\\%) but actively hurts in the Type~C regime (36.5\\% vs.\\ 37.2\\%), mirroring the regime-dependent pattern of SICA itself. Hyperparameter sensitivity (Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}): $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\\Delta > 2$\\,pp among five $K$ values, and only $T = 0.7$ among four temperatures yields a positive effect; both peaks coincide with relatively low SC baselines rather than reflecting hyperparameter effects per se. Entropy decomposition (Appendix~\\ref{app:entropy}): all SICA effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($\\Delta$ up to $+12.8$\\,pp in the intermediate-SC region), but the direction depends on the accuracy regime---high entropy signals correctable uncertainty only when the model has partial knowledge.\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test twelve remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) and three method-level (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). All nine prompt-level strategies fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\\% to 6.0\\% but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models extracting from stronger traces yield 0\\% discriminative constraints; stronger models extracting from weaker traces produce accuracy within $\\pm$1\\,pp of self-extraction. Only 1 of 4 temperature settings yields a positive effect ($p = 0.092$); multi-temperature sampling produces $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, producing near-zero average effect ($\\Delta = -1.96$\\,pp and $+1.47$\\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\\,pp on LogiQA). Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-4.50$\\,pp on LogiQA, with ${\\sim}$21\\% degenerate scores on both datasets. Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\\,pp on LogiQA ($n = 200$)---the largest degradation across all twelve strategies on both datasets. At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B. Each targets a different aspect of the independence bottleneck. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds.}\n\\label{tab:method_interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Target & FOLIO $\\Delta$ (pp) & LogiQA $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC baseline & --- & 0.00 & 0.00 \\\\\nICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96 / $+$1.47 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nContrastive & Scoring bias & $\\mathbf{-9.31}$ & $-$4.50 \\\\\nDebate & Role diversity & $\\mathbf{-18.63}$ & $\\mathbf{-23.00}$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Debate achieves the highest Eff-$K$ but collapses accuracy. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccr@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & Acc.\\ change (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nSelf-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & (baseline) \\\\\nDiverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & $\\approx$\\,0 \\\\\nDebate & 0.15 & 4.44 & $-$18.6 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",
        "numLines": 85,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 85
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/analysis.tex"
  },

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

| Contrastive | FOLIO-204 | -9.31pp | 7.37 | 0.0066 ** |

2. §5.3 Debate 段落：加 "statistically significant (p < 0.001 on both datasets)" + discordant pair 分析（FOLIO: 60 questions flipped correct→wrong vs 22 wrong→correct; LogiQA: 64 vs 18）

```text
1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	The regime-dependent pattern in \S\ref{sec:main-results} establishes \emph{where} SICA succeeds and fails. This section investigates \emph{why}: trace correlation quantifies the SC plateau (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), supporting diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}), and remediation analysis reveals a fundamental independence-accuracy trade-off (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode reaches $\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$). The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ ($K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.8\text{--}2.2$), yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence, drives the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ (Qwen conditions with $K_{\text{eff}} \leq 1.30$ never improve) and sufficient SC accuracy (Mistral on ProofWriter with the highest $K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ but SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Even when both hold, sampling variance determines whether the constraint pool is discriminative.
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
15	\label{tab:independence}
16	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
17	\toprule
18	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
19	\midrule
20	Qwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\sim}$84 & ${\sim}$0 \\
21	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\
22	Qwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\
23	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\
24	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\
25	\addlinespace
26	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\
27	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\
28	Mistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\
29	Mistral-7B (s42) & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\
30	Mistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\
31	Mistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\
32	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\
33	Mistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\
34	Mistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\
35	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\
36	\bottomrule
37	\end{tabular}
38	\end{table}
39	
40	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostic Analyses}
41	
42	Four additional analyses confirm the mechanism underlying SICA's failure. Extraction error taxonomy (Appendix~\ref{app:extraction}): 91.8\% of constraints are technically discriminative, but their discriminative direction mirrors the majority answer; removing the 8.2\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero improvement. Constraint ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}): on Mistral-7B ($n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter pooled), MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\% of predictions, with disagreements split nearly evenly (18 vs.\ 17); the solver's weighted satisfiability computation adds no value over simple constraint counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Per-domain, MAX-SAT provides a marginal edge in the intermediate-SC region (59.3\% vs.\ count-only 56.9\%) but actively hurts in the Type~C regime (36.5\% vs.\ 37.2\%), mirroring the regime-dependent pattern of SICA itself. Hyperparameter sensitivity (Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}): $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\Delta > 2$\,pp among five $K$ values, and only $T = 0.7$ among four temperatures yields a positive effect; both peaks coincide with relatively low SC baselines rather than reflecting hyperparameter effects per se. Entropy decomposition (Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}): all SICA effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($\Delta$ up to $+12.8$\,pp in the intermediate-SC region), but the direction depends on the accuracy regime---high entropy signals correctable uncertainty only when the model has partial knowledge.
43	
44	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
45	\label{sec:remediation}
46	\label{sec:interventions}
47	
48	We test twelve remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) and three method-level (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). All nine prompt-level strategies fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\% but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models extracting from stronger traces yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models extracting from weaker traces produce accuracy within $\pm$1\,pp of self-extraction. Only 1 of 4 temperature settings yields a positive effect ($p = 0.092$); multi-temperature sampling produces $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, producing near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA). Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA, with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets. Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA ($n = 200$)---the largest degradation across all twelve strategies on both datasets. At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B. Each targets a different aspect of the independence bottleneck. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds.}
56	\label{tab:method_interventions}
57	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}
58	\toprule
59	Method & Target & FOLIO $\Delta$ (pp) & LogiQA $\Delta$ (pp) \\
60	\midrule
61	SC baseline & --- & 0.00 & 0.00 \\
62	ICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96 / $+$1.47 & $-$0.50 \\
63	Contrastive & Scoring bias & $\mathbf{-9.31}$ & $-$4.50 \\
64	Debate & Role diversity & $\mathbf{-18.63}$ & $\mathbf{-23.00}$ \\
65	\bottomrule
66	\end{tabular}
67	\end{table}
68	
69	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
70

73	\small
74	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Debate achieves the highest Eff-$K$ but collapses accuracy. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}
75	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}
76	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccr@{}}
77	\toprule
78	Aggregation & $\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & Acc.\ change (pp) \\
79	\midrule
80	Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & (baseline) \\
81	Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & $\approx$\,0 \\
82	Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & $-$18.6 \\
83	\bottomrule

Each targets a different aspect of the independence bottleneck. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds.}\n\\label{tab:method_interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Target & FOLIO $\\Delta$ (pp) & LogiQA $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC baseline & --- & 0.00 & 0.00 \\\\\nICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96 / $+$1.47 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nContrastive & Scoring bias & $\\mathbf{-9.31}$ & $-$4.50 \\\\\nDebate & Role diversity & $\\mathbf{-18.63}$ & $\\mathbf{-23.00}$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Debate achieves the highest Eff-$K$ but collapses accuracy. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccr@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & Acc.\\ change (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nSelf-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & (baseline) \\\\\nDiverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & $\\approx$\\,0 \\\\\nDebate & 0.15 & 4.44 & $-$18.6 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01DqPerot8GtHGwAS1JxsHke`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The oracle experiments causally identify the failure source. Holding all pipeline components fixed and varying only constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), while the best self-extraction captures only 24.1\% of this ceiling. Because the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap localizes entirely to extraction quality.
5	
6	Trace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The \emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.
7	
8	\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\kappa$ generalization analysis (\S\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ ($\approx$ SC's $0.44$): prompt variation does not break the correlation. Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.
9	
10	\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\geq 60\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\leq 40\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.
11	
12	\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} No positive SICA effect reaches $p < 0.05$. The four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, negative effects are significant: $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$) and $-2.50$\,pp ($p = 0.011$). Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). The converging evidence rests on the pattern across analyses, not any single $p$-value.
13	
14	\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.
```

</details>

The converging evidence rests on the pattern across analyses, not any single $p$-value.",
  "new_text": "\\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} No positive SICA effect reaches $p < 0.05$. The four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, negative effects are significant: $\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp ($p = 0.014$) and $-2.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.011$). McNemar's paired test on method-level interventions confirms the pattern: three of five method-dataset conditions reach significance (Debate $p < 0.001$ on both datasets; Contrastive $p < 0.01$ on FOLIO), with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\\to$wrong flips. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). The converging evidence rests on the pattern across analyses, not any single $p$-value.",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01HPNmeS1Qzaw8UKcMC26qTW`</summary>

```text
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, producing near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets. Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. The method actively destroys SC's correct predictions rather than introducing balanced noise. Three of five method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\,$p < 0.01$.}
56	\label{tab:method_interventions}
57	\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}
58	\toprule
59	& & \multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\
60	\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \cmidrule(lr){5-6}
61	Method & Target & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
62	\midrule
63	SC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\
64	ICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\,/\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & --- \\
65	Contrastive & Scoring bias & $\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\
66	Debate & Role diversity & $\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\
67	\bottomrule
68	\end{tabular}
69	\end{table}
70	
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
72	
73	\begin{table}[t]
74	\centering
75	\small
76	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off across aggregation methods (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n = 204$). Debate achieves the highest Eff-$K$ but collapses accuracy. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}
77	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}
78	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccr@{}}
79	\toprule
80	Aggregation & $\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & Acc.\ change (pp) \\
81	\midrule
82	Self-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & (baseline) \\
83	Diverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & $\approx$\,0 \\
84	Debate & 0.15 & 4.44 & $-$18.6 \\
85	\bottomrule
86	\end{tabular}
87	\end{table}

Debate achieves the highest Eff-$K$ but collapses accuracy. $\\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}\n\\label{tab:kappa_generalization}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccr@{}}\n\\toprule\nAggregation & $\\kappa$ & Eff-$K$ & Acc.\\ change (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nSelf-Consistency & 0.44 & 2.02 & (baseline) \\\\\nDiverse-Prompt & 0.40 & 2.21 & $\\approx$\\,0 \\\\\nDebate & 0.15 & 4.44 & $-$18.6 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",
      "numLines": 38,
      "startLine": 50,
      "totalLines": 87
    }

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018D8J7pNRVoJaN6Rp49jyE8`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The oracle experiments causally identify the failure source. Holding all pipeline components fixed and varying only constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 0.001$), while the best self-extraction captures only 24.1\% of this ceiling. Because the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap localizes entirely to extraction quality.
5	
6	Trace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The \emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.
7	
8	\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\kappa$ generalization analysis (\S\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ ($\approx$ SC's $0.44$): prompt variation does not break the correlation. Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.
9	
10	\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\geq 60\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\leq 40\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.
11	
12	\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} No positive SICA effect reaches $p < 0.05$. The four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, negative effects are significant: $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$) and $-2.50$\,pp ($p = 0.011$). McNemar's paired test on method-level interventions confirms the pattern: three of five method-dataset conditions reach significance (Debate $p < 0.001$ on both datasets; Contrastive $p < 0.01$ on FOLIO), with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\to$wrong flips. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). The converging evidence rests on the pattern across analyses, not any single $p$-value.
13	
14	\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.
```

The \\emph{direct} channel limits voting diversity: Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate the CJT independence requirement, explaining the SC plateau. The \\emph{indirect} channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to vote counting (Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}). The oracle result disambiguates: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\\,pp, proving that constraint homogenization---not voting diversity---is the proximal bottleneck.\n\n\\paragraph{The independence-accuracy trade-off.} The $\\kappa$ generalization analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}) constitutes the primary finding of this paper. Diverse-Prompt achieves $\\kappa = 0.40$ ($\\approx$ SC's $0.44$): prompt variation does not break the correlation. Debate reduces $\\kappa$ to 0.15 (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.0$\\,pp on LogiQA. This independence-accuracy trade-off is a fundamental Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence necessarily degrade accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. Crucially, this trade-off constrains \\emph{all} same-model trace aggregation methods---not only symbolic approaches---because it arises from the model's output distribution, not from any specific aggregation procedure. Published systems that report positive results---DyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos}, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle}, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}---each bypass same-model self-extraction, trading simplicity for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic tools for practitioners.} Our analysis yields two zero-cost diagnostics applicable before any constraint extraction. First, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when symbolic augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\\%$ implies degeneration; SC $\\geq 60\\%$ implies confirmation bias; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ implies error amplification. Second, the $\\kappa$-based Eff-$K$ metric quantifies SC's own scaling ceiling: at Eff-$K \\approx 2$ (typical at $K = 12$), additional traces add negligible independent information regardless of aggregation method.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} No positive SICA effect reaches $p < 0.05$. The four-seed replication yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$. In contrast, negative effects are significant: $\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp ($p = 0.014$) and $-2.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.011$). McNemar's paired test on method-level interventions confirms the pattern: three of five method-dataset conditions reach significance (Debate $p < 0.001$ on both datasets; Contrastive $p < 0.01$ on FOLIO), with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\\to$wrong flips. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$). The converging evidence rests on the pattern across analyses, not any single $p$-value.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale limitations and future work.} The method-level interventions that test the trade-off most directly use Mistral-7B. At the 7B scale, Debate may impose cognitive overhead that larger models can absorb; whether 14B+ or 70B+ models shift the Pareto frontier is an open question. The trade-off itself is fundamental---it follows from the correlation structure of same-model outputs---but its severity may vary with scale. Three paths forward trade simplicity for independence: trained extractors, external provers, and multi-model pipelines.",
      "numLines": 14,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 14
    }

```text
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, producing near-zero average effect ($\Delta = -1.96$\,pp and $+1.47$\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\sim}$21\% degenerate scores on both datasets. Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. The method actively destroys SC's correct predictions rather than introducing balanced noise. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\,$p < 0.01$.}
56	\label{tab:method_interventions}
57	\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}
58	\toprule
59	& & \multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\
60	\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \cmidrule(lr){5-6}
61	Method & Target & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
62	\midrule
63	SC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\
64	ICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\,/\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\
65	Contrastive & Scoring bias & $\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\
66	Debate & Role diversity & $\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\
67	\bottomrule
68	\end{tabular}
69	\end{table}
70	
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
72	
73	\begin{table}[t]

      "content": "\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from the problem statement, producing near-zero average effect ($\\Delta = -1.96$\\,pp and $+1.47$\\,pp across two FOLIO seeds; $-0.50$\\,pp on LogiQA; McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints for each candidate but yields $\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\\,pp on LogiQA ($p = .160$, n.s.), with ${\\sim}$21\\% degenerate scores on both datasets. Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\\Delta = -18.63$\\,pp on FOLIO and $-23.00$\\,pp on LogiQA, both statistically significant by McNemar's test ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets). Discordant-pair analysis reveals the asymmetry: on FOLIO, 60 questions flipped from correct to wrong versus only 22 from wrong to correct; on LogiQA, 64 versus 18. The method actively destroys SC's correct predictions rather than introducing balanced noise. Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, confirming that degradation is systematic, not stochastic. At the 7B scale, multi-role prompting adds cognitive overhead that degrades both reasoning and extraction quality.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\\,$p < 0.01$.}\n\\label{tab:method_interventions}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}\n\\toprule\n& & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \\multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \\cmidrule(lr){5-6}\nMethod & Target & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\\\\nICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\\,/\\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\\\\nContrastive & Scoring bias & $\\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\\\\nDebate & Role diversity & $\\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across three aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Self-Consistency produces $\\kappa = 0.44$ (Eff-$K = 2.02$). Diverse-Prompt achieves $\\kappa = 0.40$ (Eff-$K = 2.21$): prompt variation does not break the underlying correlation. Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$), nearly tripling the effective sample size, but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. This constitutes a Pareto frontier: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \\citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]",

    }

1	
2	\section{Prompt Templates}
3	\label{app:prompts}
4	
5	SICA uses domain-specific prompt templates for constraint extraction: one for mathematical reasoning (GSM8K, AIME) and one for logical reasoning (ProofWriter, FOLIO). Both share the same system prompt. The LLM is called with temperature $T{=}0.3$, max tokens $1024$, and structured JSON output mode. Up to 6 traces are processed in parallel via thread pooling, with 2 retries on failure (exponential backoff, base 0.5s).
6	
7	\paragraph{System Prompt.}
8	
9	\begin{lstlisting}
10	You are a constraint extraction tool. Output ONLY
11	valid JSON. Do NOT solve problems, reason about
12	correctness, or think step-by-step. Extract
13	constraints from the given trace exactly as stated.
14	\end{lstlisting}
15	
16	\paragraph{Mathematical Constraint Extraction (EXTRACTION\_PROMPT).}
17	
18	Used for GSM8K and AIME datasets. The placeholder \texttt{\{trace\}} is replaced with the reasoning trace at runtime.
19	
20	\begin{lstlisting}
21	Extract mathematical constraints from this reasoning
22	trace as a JSON object.
23	
24	Rules:
25	- type: "equation", "inequality", or "predicate"
26	- expression: human-readable, e.g. "x + y == 14"
27	- z3_formula: a single comparison expression in
28	  Python Z3 syntax
29	- source_step: step number where the constraint
30	  appears
31	- Use variable names from the trace
32	
33	z3_formula MUST follow these rules:
34	- Allowed operators: ==, !=, <, >, <=, >=, +, -, *,
35	  /, **, %
36	- Use individual named variables: a1, a2, a3...
37	  NOT arrays a[1] or sets A
38	- Expand all sums explicitly:
39	  "a1 + a2 + a3 == 55" NOT "sum([a1,a2,a3])"
40	- NO Python builtins: sum(), len(), max(), min(),
41	  list(), set(), range(), int()
42	- Each z3_formula must be exactly ONE comparison
43	  (==, !=, <, >, <=, >=)
44	- For multiple values, write separate constraints
45	  for each
46	
47	Output ONLY the JSON below, nothing else:
48	{"constraints": [{"type": "equation",
49	  "expression": "x + y == 14",
50	  "z3_formula": "x + y == 14",
51	  "source_step": 1}],
52	 "answer": "final_answer",
53	 "variables": ["x", "y"]}
54	
55	Trace:
56	{trace}
57	\end{lstlisting}
58	
59	\paragraph{Logical Constraint Extraction (LOGIC\_EXTRACTION\_PROMPT).}
60	
61	Used for ProofWriter and FOLIO datasets.
62	
63	\begin{lstlisting}
64	IMPORTANT: You are an EXTRACTION tool, NOT a problem
65	solver.
66	- Do NOT re-solve or re-reason about the problem.
67	- Do NOT verify whether the trace's reasoning is
68	  correct.
69	- ONLY extract the logical constraints that are
70	  explicitly stated or used in the trace below.
71	- Output ONLY the JSON object, nothing else.
72	
73	Extract logical constraints from this reasoning trace
74	about a logic problem.
75	
76	The problem has facts (base assertions), rules
77	(grounded implications), and derived conclusions.
78	Convert each into a Z3 boolean formula.
79	
80	Variable naming:
81	- Unary property: property_entity
82	  (e.g., kind_anne, red_charlie, round_fiona)
83	- Binary relation: relation_subject_object
84	  (e.g., eats_bear_squirrel, likes_lion_cat)
85	- Use lowercase with underscores only
86	
87	Constraint types:
88	- "fact": a given assertion,
89	  e.g., kind_anne == True
90	- "rule": a grounded implication over specific
91	  entities, e.g., Implies(kind_anne, nice_anne)
92	- "derived": an inferred conclusion from facts +
93	  rules, e.g., nice_anne == True
94	
95	Z3 operators:
96	- Implies(a, b) for if-then rules
97	- And(a, b, ...) for conjunctive conditions
98	- Or(a, b, ...) for disjunctive conditions
99	- Not(a) for negation
100	- == True / == False for boolean assertions
101	
102	Output ONLY the JSON below, nothing else:
103	{"constraints": [{"type": "fact",
104	  "expression": "kind(anne)",
105	  "z3_formula": "kind_anne == True",
106	  "source_step": 1}],
107	 "answer": "True or False or Unknown",
108	 "variables": ["kind_anne"]}
109	
110	Trace:
111	{trace}
112	\end{lstlisting}
113	
114	\paragraph{Output Schema.}
115	
116	Both prompts produce a JSON object with the following structure:
117	
118	\begin{lstlisting}
119	{
120	  "constraints": [
121	    {
122	      "type": "&lt;equation|inequality|predicate>
123	               or &lt;fact|rule|derived>",
124	      "expression": "&lt;human-readable description>",
125	      "z3_formula": "&lt;Z3-compatible expression>",
126	      "source_step": &lt;integer>
127	    }
128	  ],
129	  "answer": "&lt;final answer string>",
130	  "variables": ["&lt;var1>", "&lt;var2>", "..."]
131	}
132	\end{lstlisting}
133	
134	The pipeline additionally injects an \texttt{answer\_assertion} constraint per trace (not produced by the LLM):
135	
136	\begin{lstlisting}
137	{"type": "answer_assertion",
138	 "expression": "answer is &lt;ans>",
139	 "z3_formula": "__sica_answer_&lt;ans> == True",
140	 "source_step": -1}
141	\end{lstlisting}
142	
143	\paragraph{Prompt Selection Logic.}
144	
145	Domain assignment is determined by dataset: FOLIO and ProofWriter use \textsc{Logic\_Extraction\_Prompt}; GSM8K, AIME, StrategyQA, and ARC-Challenge use \textsc{Extraction\_Prompt}. StrategyQA and ARC-Challenge are assigned to the mathematical prompt because their reasoning structure does not involve formal logic predicates or implications, despite StrategyQA producing boolean answers.
146	
147	
148	\section{Z3 MaxSAT Evaluation Pseudocode}
149	\label{app:z3-pseudocode}
150	
151	Algorithm~\ref{alg:maxsat} describes the Z3 MaxSAT solving procedure. Algorithm~\ref{alg:scorer} describes the source-trace attribution scoring.
152	
153	Domain constraint variables (e.g., \texttt{x + y == 14}) and answer assertion variables (e.g., \texttt{\_\_sica\_answer\_42 == True}) are logically independent in the Z3 formulation. Discriminative signal arises solely from conflicts among domain constraints: when traces supporting different answers produce mutually contradictory constraints, the solver excludes the lower-weight subset, and the scorer penalizes the corresponding answers.
154	
155	\begin{algorithm}[h]
156	\caption{Z3 Weighted Partial MAX-SAT}
157	\label{alg:maxsat}
158	\begin{algorithmic}[1]
159	\Require Deduplicated constraint set $\mathcal{C} = \{(c_m, w_m, S_m)\}_{m=1}^M$ where $c_m$ is a Z3 formula, $w_m$ is weight, $S_m$ is the set of source trace indices
160	\Ensure Partition $(\mathcal{C}^*, \mathcal{C}^{\setminus})$ where $\mathcal{C}^*$ is the max-weight satisfiable subset
161	\State $\textit{opt} \gets \textsc{Z3.Optimize()}$
162	\For{$m = 1, \ldots, M$}
163	    \State $b_m \gets$ fresh Boolean indicator
164	    \State $\textit{opt}.\textsc{Add}(b_m \Rightarrow c_m)$ \Comment{soft clause guarded by indicator}
165	    \State $\textit{opt}.\textsc{AddSoft}(b_m,\; \text{weight}=w_m)$
166	\EndFor
167	\State $\textit{result} \gets \textit{opt}.\textsc{Check}()$
168	\State $\textit{model} \gets \textit{opt}.\textsc{Model}()$
169	\State $\mathcal{C}^* \gets \{(c_m, w_m, S_m) : \textit{model}(b_m) = \top\}$
170	\State $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} \gets \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$
171	\State \Return $(\mathcal{C}^*, \mathcal{C}^{\setminus})$
172	\end{algorithmic}
173	\end{algorithm}
174	
175	\begin{algorithm}[h]
176	\caption{Source-Trace Attribution Scoring}
177	\label{alg:scorer}
178	\begin{algorithmic}[1]
179	\Require Satisfied set $\mathcal{C}^*$, excluded set $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}$, candidate answers $\mathcal{A}$, trace-answer mapping $\tau: \text{trace\_idx} \to a$, penalty weight $\alpha$
180	\Ensure Score $s(a)$ for each $a \in \mathcal{A}$
181	\For{each $a \in \mathcal{A}$}
182	    \State $T_a \gets \{i : \tau(i) = a\}$ \Comment{trace indices producing answer $a$}
183	    \State $\textit{pos}(a) \gets \sum_{(c,w,S) \in \mathcal{C}^*} w \cdot \mathbf{1}[S \cap T_a \neq \emptyset]$
184	    \State $\textit{neg}(a) \gets \sum_{(c,w,S) \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w \cdot \mathbf{1}[S \cap T_a \neq \emptyset]$
185	    \State $s(a) \gets \textit{pos}(a) - \alpha \cdot \textit{neg}(a)$
186	\EndFor
187	\State \Return $a^* = \arg\max_{a \in \mathcal{A}} s(a)$
188	\end{algorithmic}
189	\end{algorithm}
190

222	\section{Statistical Power Analysis}
223	\label{app:power}
224	
225	We compute the minimum detectable effect (MDE) for each condition at $\alpha = 0.05$ and 80\% power using exact binomial power calculations over McNemar discordant pairs. Table~\ref{tab:power} reports three grouped conditions with $n \geq 200$ (Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO and ProofWriter are pooled into a single $n{=}804$ analysis, corresponding to four Table~\ref{tab:main_results} rows), which constitute our confirmatory analyses. The remaining seven conditions ($n \leq 60$) yield infinite MDE due to insufficient discordant pairs and serve only as regime characterization.
226	
227	\begin{table}[h]
228	\centering
229	\small
230	\begin{tabular}{lrrrrr}
231	\toprule
232	\textbf{Condition} & $n$ & \textbf{MDE} & \textbf{Obs.\ $\Delta$} & \textbf{Obs.\ Pow.} \\
233	\midrule
234	Qwen-14B FOLIO+PW   & 804 & 1.86pp & $+$0.25pp & 5.7\%  \\
235	LLaMA-8B StrategyQA  & 200 & 4.64pp & $+$2.00pp & 20.6\% \\
236	LLaMA-8B FOLIO       & 204 & 4.84pp & $-$4.41pp & 18.6\% \\
237	\bottomrule
238	\end{tabular}
239	\caption{Power analysis for confirmatory conditions ($n \geq 200$). MDE = minimum detectable effect at 80\% power. All observed effects fall below their respective MDEs, and observed power remains below 21\% in every case.}
240	\label{tab:power}
241	\end{table}
242	
243	The largest study (Qwen-14B, $n{=}804$, 30 discordant pairs) can detect effects as small as 1.86pp, yet the observed $\Delta$ of $+$0.25pp is an order of magnitude smaller. The two LLaMA-8B conditions require effects of 4.64pp and 4.84pp to reach significance; neither observed $\Delta$ approaches these thresholds. All three conditions are consistent with the null hypothesis that SICA provides no accuracy gain over self-consistency.

248

The widest interval (LLaMA-8B FOLIO, [$-$5.88, $+$0.98]) skews negative, indicating that SICA may slightly hurt accuracy in this condition. These results corroborate the McNemar tests and power analysis: no condition provides evidence that symbolic constraint aggregation improves over majority voting.
